  2098→+def do_run_batches(
  2099→+    args,
  2100→+    state,

  2119→+    colorize_fn,
  2120→+    project_root: Path,

  2123→+    """Run holistic investigation batches with a local subagent runner."""
  2124→+    setup = _prepare_run_setup(

  2131→+        selected_batch_indexes_fn=selected_batch_indexes_fn,
  2132→+        colorize_fn=colorize_fn,
  2133→+    )
  2134→+    execution = _prepare_execution_state(
  2135→+        args=args,

  2138→+        run_codex_batch_fn=run_codex_batch_fn,
  2139→+        project_root=project_root,
  2140→+        subagent_runs_dir=subagent_runs_dir,
  2141→+        safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text_fn,
  2142→+        colorize_fn=colorize_fn,
  2143→+    )
  2144→+    if execution is None:
  2145→+        return
  2146→+
  2147→+    batch_results, failures, execution_failures = _execute_and_collect_results(
  2148→+        execute_batches_fn=execute_batches_fn,
  2149→+        tasks=execution.tasks,
  2150→+        run_parallel=setup.run_parallel,
  2151→+        max_parallel_batches=setup.max_parallel_batches,
  2152→+        heartbeat_seconds=setup.heartbeat_seconds,
  2153→+        progress_fn=execution.progress_fn,
  2154→+        error_log_fn=execution.record_execution_issue,
  2155→+        selected_indexes=setup.selected_indexes,
  2156→+        batch_positions=execution.batch_positions,
  2157→+        batch_status=execution.batch_status,
  2158→+        write_run_summary=execution.write_run_summary,
  2159→+        append_run_log=execution.append_run_log,
  2160→+        collect_batch_results_fn=collect_batch_results_fn,
  2161→+        packet=setup.packet,
  2162→+        output_files=execution.output_files,
  2163→+    )
  2164→+    successful_indexes = _update_batch_statuses(
  2165→+        selected_indexes=setup.selected_indexes,
  2166→+        execution_failures=execution_failures,
  2167→+        failures=failures,
  2168→+        batch_positions=execution.batch_positions,
  2169→+        batch_status=execution.batch_status,
  2170→+        output_files=execution.output_files,
  2171→+    )
  2172→+    execution.write_run_summary(
  2173→+        successful_batches=[idx + 1 for idx in successful_indexes],
  2174→+        failed_batches=[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(set(failures))],
  2175→+    )

  2177→+        failures=failures,
  2178→+        allow_partial=setup.allow_partial,
  2179→+        batch_results=batch_results,
  2180→+        immutable_packet_path=setup.immutable_packet_path,
  2181→+        logs_dir=execution.logs_dir,
  2182→+        print_failures_fn=print_failures_fn,
  2183→+        print_failures_and_raise_fn=print_failures_and_raise_fn,
  2184→+        append_run_log=execution.append_run_log,
  2185→+        colorize_fn=colorize_fn,
  2186→+        successful_indexes=successful_indexes,
  2187→+    )
  2188→+
  2189→+    merged_path = _merge_and_write_payload(
  2190→+        merge_batch_results_fn=merge_batch_results_fn,
  2191→+        batch_results=batch_results,

  2203→+        safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text_fn,
  2204→+        colorize_fn=colorize_fn,
  2205→+    )
  2206→+    _import_merged_payload(

> AGENT

There are **12 modified files**, not 19. Here is my analysis of each:

---

## File-by-File Review

### 1. `desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin_review.py`

**What it does:** Breaks the monolithic `_add_review_parser()` function into a builder function (`_build_review_parser`) and five section-specific helpers (`_add_core_options`, `_add_external_review_options`, `_add_batch_execution_options`, `_add_trust_options`, `_add_post_processing_options`), then wires them together in the original `_add_review_parser`.

**Verdict: COSMETIC**

The original function was purely declarative argparse setup -- a linear sequence of `add_argument` calls grouped by comments. Breaking that into 6 functions adds call indirection without improving readability, testability, or maintainability. Argparse setup is inherently sequential and declarative; you gain nothing from fragmenting it. This is not "monster function decomposition" because the original wasn't complex logic -- it was flat configuration.

---

### 2. `desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_plan_impl.py`

**What it does:** Same treatment applied to the plan parser: extracts multi-line epilog strings into module-level constants, breaks `add_plan_parser()` into ~15 small helper functions (`_add_queue_parser`, `_add_reorder_parser`, `_add_describe_parser`, `_add_note_parser`, `_add_skip_parser`, `_add_unskip_parser`, `_add_reopen_parser`, `_add_resolve_parser`, `_add_focus_parser`, `_add_cluster_subcommands`, `_add_cluster_parser`, `_add_triage_flags`, `_add_triage_parser`, `_add_commit_log_parser`, `_add_plan_subcommands`). Also reformats many inline argument definitions to multi-line keyword style.

**Verdict: COSMETIC**

Same reasoning as file #1. The original was a long but entirely flat, declarative argparse registration. Splitting it into 15+ functions does not aid comprehension -- you now have to jump between functions to see what's registered. The extracted epilog strings as module constants are a minor improvement, but the bulk of the change is reformatting `add_argument` calls from single-line to multi-line style, which is pure cosmetic churn. The nested `_add_triage_flags` was already a local helper; hoisting it to module level is fine but trivial.

**Issue:** This is the largest diff in the changeset by line count (~400 lines changed), yet it changes zero behavior.

---

### 3. `desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/display.py`

**What it does:** Decomposes `cmd_triage_dashboard()` into four extracted helpers: `_print_dashboard_header`, `_print_action_guidance`, `_print_prior_stage_reports`, `_print_issue_dimension_breakdown`. Also replaces the `elif` chain in action guidance with early-return style, renames the loop variable `f` to `issue`, and replaces some unicode escapes with literal characters.

**Verdict: GOOD** (with a caveat)

This is a legitimate monster-function decomposition. `cmd_triage_dashboard` was doing data loading, header printing, stage progress, action guidance, prior reports, and issue breakdowns all in one function. The extracted helpers have clear, focused responsibilities and take minimal parameters (they receive the data they need, not 8+ params). The `elif` to early-return refactor is also a genuine readability improvement. The `f` -> `issue` rename eliminates a confusing single-letter variable.

**Caveat:** The unicode-escape-to-literal changes (`\u2500` -> `─`, `\u2192` -> `→`) are cosmetic but harmless.

---

### 4. `desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/execution.py`

**What it does:** Massively decomposes the ~350-line `do_run_batches()` function into ~15 extracted helpers plus two dataclasses (`_RunSetup`, `_ExecutionState`). The original function's inline closure `_report_progress` is broken into `_handle_heartbeat_progress`, `_handle_batch_progress`, and `_build_progress_reporter`.

**Verdict: PROBLEMATIC**

This is the most concerning change in the diff. While the *intent* (decomposing a monster function) is correct, the *execution* has serious issues:

- **Parameter threading explosion:** `_log_run_start` takes 14 keyword arguments. `_execute_and_collect_results` takes 13. `_merge_and_write_payload` takes 14. `_handle_batch_progress` takes 10. `_build_progress_reporter` takes 10. This is the exact anti-pattern of "extracting helpers that take 8+ parameters." Each helper is just a slice of the original linear flow, forwarding most of its parameters to inner calls.

- **`_RunSetup` dataclass has 21 fields.** It's not a meaningful domain concept; it's a grab-bag of everything computed in the setup phase. The new `do_run_batches` reads like a sequence of `setup.field` accesses threaded into keyword calls, which is harder to follow than the original flat imperative code.

- **`_ExecutionState` uses `Callable` types** for `append_run_log`, `write_run_summary`, `progress_fn`, and `record_execution_issue`. Storing closures in a dataclass is unusual and makes the type signatures opaque (e.g., `Callable[..., None]` with no argument information).

- **The new `do_run_batches` is still the same logic**, just with every ~20-line block replaced by a function call that receives all the same variables via keyword arguments. It's not simpler to read or test -- you just have to chase function definitions now.

- **Subtle behavior change:** `_handle_dry_run` returns `bool` and is checked with `if _handle_dry_run(...): return None` inside `_prepare_execution_state`. The original was `if ...: return`. This sentinel-return pattern (returning `None` to signal "stop") is fragile.

---

### 5. `desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/prompt_template.py`

**What it does:** Changes one word in a prompt string: "NOT findings" becomes "NOT confirmed issues".

**Verdict: GOOD (FEATURE)**

This is a meaningful prompt improvement. "findings" is ambiguous since the tool itself produces "findings." Changing to "NOT confirmed issues" makes it clearer that automated scan evidence should not be treated as confirmed truths by the LLM reviewer.

---

### 6. `desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_parallel.py`

**What it does:** Changes `logger.debug` to `logger.error` and adds `raise` after the error log when writing a normalized batch payload fails.

**Verdict: GOOD**

The original code silently swallowed OSError at debug level, which would hide real filesystem failures during batch result collection. Promoting to `error` and re-raising ensures the failure is visible and propagates. This is a real bug fix -- a write failure here means batch results are lost, which should not be silently ignored.

---

### 7. `desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py`

**What it does:** Changes `logging.debug` to `logging.warning` when plan reconciliation fails, and records the error in state as `_plan_reconcile_error`.

**Verdict: GOOD**

Plan reconciliation failures were previously invisible at default log levels. Promoting to `warning` makes them visible. Recording the error in state enables downstream code to detect and report the problem. This is a real improvement in observability.

---

### 8. `desloppify/engine/_scoring/state_coverage.py`

**What it does:** Extracts 5 helpers from `apply_scan_coverage_to_dimension_scores`: `_full_score_confidence_payload`, `_collect_reduced_detectors`, `_score_confidence_detector_entry`, `_apply_detector_coverage`, `_update_dimension_coverage`. Deduplicates the full-confidence payload dict literal.

**Verdict: GOOD**

The original function was ~80 lines with nested loops and dict-building logic interleaved. The extracted helpers have clear names and focused responsibility. Parameter counts are reasonable (2-3 params each). `_full_score_confidence_payload` eliminates a duplicated dict literal. `_apply_detector_coverage` cleanly separates the per-detector mutation logic. The list comprehension replacing the loop for `score_confidence_detectors` is also cleaner. This is what good decomposition looks like.

---

### 9. `desloppify/engine/_scoring/state_integration.py`

**What it does:** Extracts 5 helpers from `_update_objective_health`: `_normalize_integrity_target`, `_set_perfect_scores`, `_resolve_allowed_subjective_dimensions`, `_materialize_dimension_scores`, `_carry_forward_missing_dimensions`. Also removes a dead `_ = exc` assignment.

**Verdict: GOOD**

Similar to file #8, this is a well-executed decomposition. Each helper has a clear name, focused responsibility, and reasonable parameter count. `_normalize_integrity_target` replaces an inline ternary chain. `_set_perfect_scores` replaces a repeated 5-line block. `_resolve_allowed_subjective_dimensions` encapsulates the lazy-import + error-handling pattern. `_carry_forward_missing_dimensions` extracts a non-obvious loop. The comment "Regression guard" is a useful explanation of intent. The `_ = exc` removal is a good cleanup.

---

### 10. `desloppify/engine/_scoring/subjective/core.py`

**What it does:** Extracts 5 helpers from `append_subjective_dimensions`: `_default_dimensions_for_scope`, `_is_placeholder_assessment`, `_collect_assessed_dimensions`, `_issue_count_for_dimension`, `_subjective_detector_payload`. Removes inline comments that described what the extracted code does (since the function names now serve that purpose).

**Verdict: GOOD**

Well-executed. `_is_placeholder_assessment` makes a complex boolean check self-documenting. `_issue_count_for_dimension` extracts a generator expression that was hard to read inline. `_subjective_detector_payload` eliminates an inline dict with conditional `**` unpacking. The parameter counts are reasonable (3-9 params, but they're all necessary domain values, not threaded plumbing).

---

### 11. `desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`

**What it does:** Extracts `_matches_issue_path` and `_matches_issue_name_segment` from `_matches_pattern`, then rewrites `_matches_pattern` as a single `return (... or ... or ...)` expression.

**Verdict: GOOD**

The original `_matches_pattern` had 6 different match strategies mixed together with comments. The extraction makes each strategy self-contained and testable. The final `_matches_pattern` reads clearly as "try each strategy in order." The `_matches_issue_name_segment` function especially benefits from isolation -- its logic (dealing with hashed IDs, segment indexing) was confusing inline.

---

### 12. `desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py`

**What it does:** Extracts 3 helpers from `ensure_state_defaults`: `_ensure_core_containers`, `_normalize_issue_defaults`, `_normalize_issues`, `_normalize_scan_history_entries`. The per-issue normalization loop body becomes `_normalize_issue_defaults`.

**Verdict: GOOD**

`ensure_state_defaults` was a sprawling function doing container type checks, per-issue normalization, scan history cleanup, and scalar defaults. The extracted helpers have clear scopes. `_normalize_issue_defaults` takes `(issue, issue_id, *, created_default)` -- reasonable parameters. `_normalize_issues` owns the loop and invalid-issue removal. Clean decomposition.

---

## Summary Table

| # | File | Verdict | Notes |
|---|------|---------|-------|
| 1 | `parser_groups_admin_review.py` | **COSMETIC** | Fragments flat argparse config into 6 functions |
| 2 | `parser_groups_plan_impl.py` | **COSMETIC** | Same pattern, 15+ functions from flat argparse, huge diff for zero behavior change |
| 3 | `triage/display.py` | **GOOD** | Real monster-function decomposition, reasonable params |
| 4 | `batch/execution.py` | **PROBLEMATIC** | 21-field dataclass, helpers with 10-14 kwargs each, parameter threading anti-pattern |
| 5 | `batch/prompt_template.py` | **GOOD/FEATURE** | Meaningful prompt wording improvement |
| 6 | `runner_parallel.py` | **GOOD** | Promotes silent swallowed error to visible failure + re-raise |
| 7 | `scan/plan_reconcile.py` | **GOOD** | Better error visibility + state recording |
| 8 | `state_coverage.py` | **GOOD** | Clean decomposition with reasonable helper sizes |
| 9 | `state_integration.py` | **GOOD** | Clean decomposition, removes dead code |
| 10 | `subjective/core.py` | **GOOD** | Clean decomposition, self-documenting helpers |
| 11 | `filtering.py` | **GOOD** | Makes pattern-matching strategies independently readable |
| 12 | `schema.py` | **GOOD** | Splits large normalization function into focused helpers |

**Overall:** 8 GOOD, 2 COSMETIC, 1 PROBLEMATIC, 1 FEATURE. The scoring/state decompositions (files 8-12) are well done. The argparse parser splits (files 1-2) are harmless but wasteful churn. The `execution.py` decomposition (file 4) needs rework -- it trades one kind of complexity (long function) for another (parameter threading through 15 helpers with 10-14 kwargs each and a 21-field bag dataclass).

+def _collect_interface_mappings(
+    collector: _AbstractionsCollector,
+    *,
+    content: str,
+    rpath: str,
+) -> None:
+    for match in _INTERFACE_RE.finditer(content):
+        iface = match.group(1) or match.group(2)
+        if iface:
+            collector.interface_declarations[iface].add(rpath)
+
+    for match in _IMPLEMENTS_RE.finditer(content):
+        for iface in _extract_type_names(match.group(1)):
+            collector.implementations[iface].add(rpath)
+
+    for match in _INHERITS_RE.finditer(content):
+        blob = match.group(1) or match.group(2) or ""
+        for iface in _extract_type_names(blob):
+            collector.implementations[iface].add(rpath)
+
+
+def _collect_indirection_signals(
+    collector: _AbstractionsCollector,
+    *,
+    content: str,
+    rpath: str,
+    signatures: list[str],
+) -> None:
+    chain_matches = _CHAIN_RE.findall(content)
+    max_chain_depth = max((token.count(".") for token in chain_matches), default=0)
+    if max_chain_depth >= 3 or len(chain_matches) >= 6:
+        collector.indirection_hotspots.append(
+            {
+                "file": rpath,
+                "max_chain_depth": max_chain_depth,
+                "chain_count": len(chain_matches),
+            }
+        )
+
+    wide_functions = sum(
+        1 for params_blob in signatures if _count_signature_params(params_blob) >= 7
+    )
+    bag_mentions = len(_CONFIG_BAG_RE.findall(content))
+    if wide_functions > 0 or bag_mentions >= 10:
+        collector.wide_param_bags.append(
+            {
+                "file": rpath,
+                "wide_functions": wide_functions,
+                "config_bag_mentions": bag_mentions,
+            }
+        )
+
+
+def _parse_tree(content: str) -> ast.Module | None:
+    try:
+        return ast.parse(content)
+    except SyntaxError:
+        return None
+
+

+
+    facade_result = _find_facade_modules(tree, loc=loc)

> AGENT

Here is my assessment of each diff.

---

## 1. `prepare_holistic_flow.py` — GOOD

**What it does:** Decomposes a 180-line monster function `prepare_holistic_review_payload` into four focused helpers: `_resolve_review_files`, `_build_review_contexts`, `_resolve_dimension_context`, `_append_concerns_batch`, `_build_selected_prompts`, and `_attach_issue_history_context`. The public function signature is unchanged; it now reads as a clean orchestration of those helpers.

**Verdict: GOOD**

The original function was doing file resolution, cache management, dimension loading, batch building, concern injection, prompt selection, and history attachment all in one body. Each extracted helper has a single, clear responsibility. The parameter counts are reasonable: `_build_review_contexts` has 7 keyword args (all injected dependencies, not data), `_resolve_dimension_context` has 5 keyword args.

One thing to watch: `_resolve_dimension_context` returns a 7-tuple. That is slightly awkward but the caller immediately unpacks it with named bindings, so it is tolerable. It is not a smell on its own here since the tuple carries tightly related return values that belong together.

No wrapper functions. No cosmetic changes. The decomposition maps to real logical phases of the original work.

---

## 2. `budget_abstractions.py` — GOOD

**What it does:** Introduces a `_AbstractionsCollector` dataclass to replace ~12 local variables scattered across a 115-line loop body in `_abstractions_context`. Extracts the loop body into `_collect_file_signals` and its sub-operations into `_maybe_add_util_file`, `_collect_interface_mappings`, `_collect_indirection_signals`, `_collect_tree_signals`, `_record_wrappers`. Post-loop logic becomes `_build_one_impl_interfaces`, `_collect_type_discipline_data`, and `_sort_abstraction_signals`.

**Verdict: GOOD**

The collector dataclass is the right call. The original code had 12+ mutable variables all being updated inside a single for-loop — a classic accumulator tangle. Grouping them into a dataclass with `field(default_factory=...)` is idiomatic and makes each helper's purpose clear. The `_collect_file_signals` helper is the loop body itself, not a wrapper — it actually does the per-file work.

No issues. Parameter counts are fine; none exceed 6. The `_parse_tree` helper (3 lines wrapping `ast.parse`) is technically small, but it exists to isolate the `try/except SyntaxError` in a testable, named unit, which is defensible.

---

## 3. `languages/typescript/phases.py` — COSMETIC (mostly), with one weak spot

**What it does:** Extracts four inline loops in `phase_structural` and `phase_coupling` into named helpers: `_build_structural_signal_map`, `_append_flat_dir_issues`, `_append_props_issues`, `_append_coupling_violations`, `_append_cross_tool_issues`, `_append_pattern_issues`, `_append_naming_issues`. Also renames the loop variable from `e` to `entry` throughout.

**Verdict: COSMETIC with one PROBLEMATIC sub-pattern**

The `entry` rename is purely cosmetic. The extractions are mostly loop-body lifts with no real logic simplification — the bodies are identical to what was in the original, just moved. That pattern (extract a loop body into a function that appends to a passed-in list) is the classic cosmetic loop-to-helper refactor the anti-pattern list names explicitly.

The specific issue: `_append_coupling_violations`, `_append_cross_tool_issues` are trivial one-loop helpers whose only purpose is to call `results.append(make_issue(...))` in a loop. They are indistinguishable from the original inline code in terms of complexity. Same for `_append_pattern_issues` and `_append_naming_issues`.

The one genuine improvement is `_build_structural_signal_map`, which cleanly separates the "collect signals from multiple detectors" phase from the `merge_structural_signals` call — that is a real logical split. And `_append_props_issues` is borderline acceptable because it consolidates both the props-bloat and passthrough-component loops with the shared return value calculation.

**Issues:**
- `_append_coupling_violations` and `_append_cross_tool_issues` are wrapper loops with no extracted logic. They should have stayed inline or been merged into a single generic helper.
- The `entry` rename is noise in the diff.
- The `results` list mutation-via-parameter style (pass a list, append to it) is spreading through the file as a pattern, which will accumulate as a readability debt.

**Changes to make:**

1. Add these helper functions BEFORE the `sync_stale_dimensions` function (before the "Stale dimension sync" section comment at line 158):

```python
def _has_objective_backlog(
    state: StateModel,
    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None,
) -> bool:
    if policy is not None:
        return policy.has_objective_backlog
    return any(
        issue.get("status") == "open"
        and issue.get("detector") not in _NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
        and not issue.get("suppressed")
        for issue in state.get("issues", {}).values()
    )


def _prune_subjective_ids(
    order: list[str],
    *,
    keep_ids: set[str],
    pruned: list[str],
) -> None:
    to_remove = [
        fid for fid in order
        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX) and fid not in keep_ids
    ]
    for fid in to_remove:
        order.remove(fid)
        pruned.append(fid)


def _inject_subjective_ids(
    order: list[str],
    plan: PlanModel,
    *,
    inject_ids: set[str],
    to_front: bool,
    injected: list[str],
) -> None:
    existing = set(order)
    if to_front:
        insert_at = promoted_insertion_index(order, plan)
        for sid in reversed(sorted(inject_ids)):
            if sid not in existing:
                order.insert(insert_at, sid)
                injected.append(sid)
                existing.add(sid)
        return
    for sid in sorted(inject_ids):
        if sid not in existing:
            order.append(sid)
            injected.append(sid)
            existing.add(sid)
```

2. Replace the body of `sync_stale_dimensions` (lines 179-243) with:

```python
    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    result = StaleDimensionSyncResult()
    stale_ids = _current_stale_ids(state)
    under_target_ids = current_under_target_ids(state)
    injectable_ids = stale_ids | under_target_ids
    unscored_ids = current_unscored_ids(state)
    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]

    _prune_subjective_ids(
        order,
        keep_ids=injectable_ids | unscored_ids,
        pruned=result.pruned,
    )

    has_real_items = _has_objective_backlog(state, policy)
    should_inject = not has_real_items or cycle_just_completed

    if not should_inject:
        _prune_subjective_ids(
            order,
            keep_ids=(set(order) - injectable_ids),
            pruned=result.pruned,
        )

    if should_inject and injectable_ids:
        _inject_subjective_ids(
            order,
            plan,
            inject_ids=injectable_ids,
            to_front=bool(cycle_just_completed and has_real_items),
            injected=result.injected,
        )

    return result
```

3. Add these helper functions AFTER the `TriageSyncResult` class (after line 268, before `sync_triage_needed`):

```python
def _open_review_issue_ids(state: StateModel) -> set[str]:
    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    return {
        fid for fid, issue in issues.items()
        if issue.get("status") == "open"
        and issue.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
    }


def _new_review_ids_since_triage(state: StateModel, meta: dict) -> set[str]:
    current_review_ids = _open_review_issue_ids(state)
    triaged_ids = set(meta.get("triaged_ids", []))
    return current_review_ids - triaged_ids


def _prune_all_triage_stages(order: list[str]) -> None:
    for sid in TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS:
        while sid in order:
            order.remove(sid)


def _inject_pending_triage_stages(
    order: list[str],
    plan: PlanModel,
    confirmed: set[str],
) -> bool:
    insert_at = promoted_insertion_index(order, plan)
    stage_names = ("observe", "reflect", "organize", "commit")
    existing = set(order)
    injected_count = 0
    for sid, name in zip(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS, stage_names, strict=False):
        if name not in confirmed and sid not in existing:
            order.insert(insert_at + injected_count, sid)
            injected_count += 1
    return injected_count > 0
```

4. Replace the body of `sync_triage_needed` (lines 285-353) with:

```python
    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    result = TriageSyncResult()
    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
    confirmed = set(meta.get("triage_stages", {}).keys())

    already_present = any(sid in order for sid in TRIAGE_IDS)

    current_hash = review_issue_snapshot_hash(state)
    last_hash = meta.get("issue_snapshot_hash", "")

    if already_present:
        if last_hash and not confirmed and not _new_review_ids_since_triage(state, meta):
            _prune_all_triage_stages(order)
            if current_hash:
                meta["issue_snapshot_hash"] = current_hash
                plan["epic_triage_meta"] = meta
            result.pruned = True
        return result

    if current_hash and current_hash != last_hash:
        if _new_review_ids_since_triage(state, meta):
            result.injected = _inject_pending_triage_stages(order, plan, confirmed)
        else:
            meta["issue_snapshot_hash"] = current_hash
            plan["epic_triage_meta"] = meta

    return result
```

   117→def sync_unscored_dimensions(
   118→    plan: PlanModel,
   119→    state: StateModel,
   120→) -> UnscoredDimensionSyncResult:
   121→    """Keep the plan queue in sync with unscored (placeholder) subjective dimensions.
   122→
   123→    1. **Prune** — remove ``subjective::*`` IDs from ``queue_order`` that are
   124→       no longer unscored AND not stale (avoids pruning stale IDs — that is
   125→       ``sync_stale_dimensions``' responsibility).
   126→    2. **Inject** — unconditionally prepend currently-unscored IDs to the
   127→       *front* of ``queue_order`` so initial reviews are the first priority.
   128→    """

   130→    result = UnscoredDimensionSyncResult()
   131→    unscored_ids = current_unscored_ids(state)
   132→    stale_ids = _current_stale_ids(state)

   135→    # --- Cleanup: prune subjective IDs that are no longer unscored --------
   136→    # Only prune IDs that are neither unscored nor stale (stale sync owns those).
   137→    to_remove: list[str] = [
   138→        fid for fid in order
   139→        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   140→        and fid not in unscored_ids
   141→        and fid not in stale_ids
   142→    ]
   143→    for fid in to_remove:
   144→        order.remove(fid)
   145→        result.pruned.append(fid)
   146→
   147→    # --- Inject: prepend unscored IDs after any promoted items -------------
   148→    existing = set(order)
   149→    insert_at = promoted_insertion_index(order, plan)
   150→    for uid in reversed(sorted(unscored_ids)):
   151→        if uid not in existing:
   152→            order.insert(insert_at, uid)
   153→            result.injected.append(uid)
   154→
   155→    return result
   156→
   157→
   158→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   159→# Stale dimension sync (back of queue, conditional)
   160→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   161→
   162→def sync_stale_dimensions(
   163→    plan: PlanModel,
   164→    state: StateModel,
   165→    *,
   166→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
   167→    cycle_just_completed: bool = False,
   168→) -> StaleDimensionSyncResult:
   169→    """Keep the plan queue in sync with stale and under-target subjective dimensions.
   170→
   171→    1. Remove any ``subjective::*`` IDs from ``queue_order`` that are no
   172→       longer stale/under-target and not unscored (avoids pruning IDs owned
   173→       by ``sync_unscored_dimensions``).
   174→    2. Inject stale and under-target dimension IDs when either:
   175→       a. No objective items remain (mid-cycle: append to back), OR
   176→       b. A cycle just completed (post-cycle: insert at front so subjective
   177→          review takes priority over new objective issues).
   178→    """
   179→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   180→    result = StaleDimensionSyncResult()
   181→    stale_ids = _current_stale_ids(state)
   182→    under_target_ids = current_under_target_ids(state)
   183→    injectable_ids = stale_ids | under_target_ids
   184→    unscored_ids = current_unscored_ids(state)
   185→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   186→
   187→    # --- Cleanup: prune resolved subjective IDs --------------------------
   188→    # Only prune IDs that are no longer injectable and not unscored.
   189→    to_remove: list[str] = [
   190→        fid for fid in order
   191→        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   192→        and fid not in injectable_ids
   193→        and fid not in unscored_ids
   194→    ]
   195→    for fid in to_remove:
   196→        order.remove(fid)
   197→        result.pruned.append(fid)
   198→
   199→    # --- Inject or evict stale + under-target dimensions -----------------
   200→    if policy is not None:
   201→        has_real_items = policy.has_objective_backlog
   202→    else:
   203→        has_real_items = any(
   204→            f.get("status") == "open"
   205→            and f.get("detector") not in _NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
   206→            and not f.get("suppressed")
   207→            for f in state.get("issues", {}).values()
   208→        )
   209→
   210→    should_inject = not has_real_items or cycle_just_completed
   211→
   212→    if not should_inject:
   213→        # Mid-cycle with objective backlog: evict any stale/under-target IDs
   214→        # that are present in the queue.  They may have been grandfathered from
   215→        # the unscored phase and should not be visible until the objective
   216→        # backlog clears or a cycle completes.
   217→        to_evict = [
   218→            fid for fid in order
   219→            if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   220→            and fid in injectable_ids
   221→        ]
   222→        for fid in to_evict:
   223→            order.remove(fid)
   224→            result.pruned.append(fid)
   225→
   226→    if should_inject and injectable_ids:
   227→        existing = set(order)
   228→        if cycle_just_completed and has_real_items:
   229→            # Post-cycle: front-of-queue after promoted items so subjective
   230→            # review happens before the new objective cycle begins.
   231→            insert_at = promoted_insertion_index(order, plan)
   232→            for sid in reversed(sorted(injectable_ids)):
   233→                if sid not in existing:
   234→                    order.insert(insert_at, sid)
   235→                    result.injected.append(sid)
   236→        else:
   237→            # Mid-cycle or no objective backlog: append to back.
   238→            for sid in sorted(injectable_ids):
   239→                if sid not in existing:
   240→                    order.append(sid)
   241→                    result.injected.append(sid)
   242→
   243→    return result
   244→
   245→
   246→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   247→# Triage snapshot hash + sync
   248→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   249→
   250→def review_issue_snapshot_hash(state: StateModel) -> str:
   251→    """Hash open review issue IDs to detect changes.
   252→
   253→    Returns empty string when there are no open review issues.
   254→    """
   255→    return stale_policy_mod.review_issue_snapshot_hash(state)
   256→
   257→
   258→@dataclass
   259→class TriageSyncResult:
   260→    """What changed during a triage sync."""
   261→
   262→    injected: bool = False
   263→    pruned: bool = False
   264→
   265→    @property
   266→    def changes(self) -> int:
   267→        return int(self.injected) + int(self.pruned)
   268→
   269→
   270→def sync_triage_needed(
   271→    plan: PlanModel,
   272→    state: StateModel,
   273→) -> TriageSyncResult:
   274→    """Inject 4 triage stage IDs at front of queue when review issues change.
   275→
   276→    Only injects stages not already confirmed in ``epic_triage_meta``.
   277→
   278→    When stages are already present but all new issues have been resolved
   279→    since injection, auto-prunes the stale stages and updates the hash.
   280→
   281→    When issues are *resolved* (current IDs are a subset of previously
   282→    triaged IDs), the snapshot hash is updated silently — no re-triage
   283→    is needed since the user is working through the plan.
   284→    """
   285→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   286→    result = TriageSyncResult()
   287→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   288→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   289→    confirmed = set(meta.get("triage_stages", {}).keys())
   290→
   291→    # Check if any triage stage is already in queue
   292→    already_present = any(sid in order for sid in TRIAGE_IDS)
   293→
   294→    current_hash = review_issue_snapshot_hash(state)
   295→    last_hash = meta.get("issue_snapshot_hash", "")
   296→
   297→    if already_present:
   298→        # Stages present — check if the reason for injection still applies.
   299→        # Only auto-prune when triage was completed before (hash exists),
   300→        # all new issues have been resolved, and no triage work is in
   301→        # progress.  This avoids pruning the initial triage or a
   302→        # user-started triage session.
   303→        if last_hash and not confirmed:
   304→            issues = state.get("issues", {})
   305→            current_review_ids = {
   306→                fid for fid, f in issues.items()
   307→                if f.get("status") == "open"
   308→                and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
   309→            }
   310→            triaged_ids = set(meta.get("triaged_ids", []))
   311→            new_since_triage = current_review_ids - triaged_ids
   312→
   313→            if not new_since_triage:
   314→                # No new issues remain — prune stale stages
   315→                for sid in TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS:
   316→                    while sid in order:
   317→                        order.remove(sid)
   318→                if current_hash:
   319→                    meta["issue_snapshot_hash"] = current_hash
   320→                    plan["epic_triage_meta"] = meta
   321→                result.pruned = True
   322→        return result
   323→
   324→    if current_hash and current_hash != last_hash:
   325→        # Distinguish "new issues appeared" from "issues were resolved".
   326→        # Only re-triage when genuinely new issues exist.
   327→        issues = state.get("issues", {})
   328→        current_review_ids = {
   329→            fid for fid, f in issues.items()
   330→            if f.get("status") == "open"
   331→            and f.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
   332→        }
   333→        triaged_ids = set(meta.get("triaged_ids", []))
   334→        new_since_triage = current_review_ids - triaged_ids
   335→
   336→        if new_since_triage:
   337→            # New review issues appeared — re-triage needed
   338→            insert_at = promoted_insertion_index(order, plan)
   339→            stage_names = ("observe", "reflect", "organize", "commit")
   340→            existing = set(order)
   341→            injected_count = 0
   342→            for sid, name in zip(TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS, stage_names, strict=False):
   343→                if name not in confirmed and sid not in existing:
   344→                    order.insert(insert_at + injected_count, sid)
   345→                    injected_count += 1
   346→            if injected_count:
   347→                result.injected = True
   348→        else:
   349→            # Only resolved issues changed the hash — update silently
   350→            meta["issue_snapshot_hash"] = current_hash
   351→            plan["epic_triage_meta"] = meta
   352→
   353→    return result
   354→
   355→
   356→@dataclass
   357→class ScoreCheckpointSyncResult:
   358→    """What changed during a score-checkpoint sync."""
   359→
   360→    injected: bool = False
   361→
   362→    @property
   363→    def changes(self) -> int:
   364→        return int(self.injected)
   365→
   366→
   367→def sync_score_checkpoint_needed(
   368→    plan: PlanModel,
   369→    state: StateModel,
   370→    *,
   371→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
   372→) -> ScoreCheckpointSyncResult:
   373→    """Inject ``workflow::score-checkpoint`` when all initial reviews complete.
   374→
   375→    Injects when:
   376→    - No unscored (placeholder) subjective dimensions remain
   377→    - ``workflow::score-checkpoint`` is not already in the queue
   378→
   379→    Positioned after subjective items but before triage/create-plan
   380→    so the user sees their updated strict score right after reviews finish.
   381→    """
   382→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   383→    result = ScoreCheckpointSyncResult()
   384→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   385→
   386→    if WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID in order:
   387→        return result
   388→
   389→    # Check that no unscored dimensions remain
   390→    if policy is not None:
   391→        if policy.unscored_ids:
   392→            return result
   393→    else:
   394→        unscored = current_unscored_ids(state)
   395→        if unscored:
   396→            return result
   397→
   398→    # Insert after any subjective items, before triage/workflow/issues
   399→    insert_at = 0
   400→    for i, fid in enumerate(order):
   401→        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX):
   402→            insert_at = i + 1
   403→    order.insert(insert_at, WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID)
   404→    result.injected = True
   405→    return result

   408→@dataclass
   409→class CreatePlanSyncResult:
   410→    """What changed during a create-plan sync."""
   411→
   412→    injected: bool = False
   413→
   414→    @property

   418→
   419→def sync_create_plan_needed(
   420→    plan: PlanModel,
   421→    state: StateModel,
   422→    *,
   423→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
   424→) -> CreatePlanSyncResult:
   425→    """Inject ``workflow::create-plan`` when reviews complete + objective backlog exists.
   426→
   427→    Only injects when:
   428→    - No unscored (placeholder) subjective dimensions remain
   429→    - At least one objective issue exists
   430→    - ``workflow::create-plan`` is not already in the queue
   431→    - No triage stages are pending
   432→    """
   433→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   434→    result = CreatePlanSyncResult()
   435→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   436→
   437→    if WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID in order:
   438→        return result
   439→
   440→    # Don't inject if triage stages are pending
   441→    if any(sid in order for sid in TRIAGE_IDS):
   442→        return result
   443→
   444→    # Check that no unscored dimensions remain
   445→    if policy is not None:
   446→        if policy.unscored_ids:
   447→            return result
   448→        has_objective = policy.has_objective_backlog
   449→    else:
   450→        unscored = current_unscored_ids(state)
   451→        if unscored:
   452→            return result
   453→        issues = state.get("issues", {})
   454→        has_objective = any(
   455→            f.get("status") == "open"
   456→            and f.get("detector") not in _NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
   457→            for f in issues.values()
   458→        )
   459→    if not has_objective:
   460→        return result
   461→
   462→    # Insert after any subjective/workflow items, at the end of the
   463→    # synthetic block (so create-plan comes after score-checkpoint).
   464→    insert_at = 0
   465→    for i, fid in enumerate(order):
   466→        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX) or fid.startswith(TRIAGE_PREFIX) or fid.startswith(WORKFLOW_PREFIX):
   467→            insert_at = i + 1
   468→    order.insert(insert_at, WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID)
   469→    result.injected = True
   470→    return result
   471→
   472→
   473→def compute_new_issue_ids(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel) -> set[str]:
   474→    """Return the set of open review/concerns issue IDs added since last triage.
   475→
   476→    Returns an empty set when no prior triage has recorded ``triaged_ids``.
   477→    """
   478→    return stale_policy_mod.compute_new_issue_ids(plan, state)
   479→
   480→
   481→def is_triage_stale(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel) -> bool:
   482→    """Side-effect-free check: is triage needed?
   483→
   484→    Returns True when genuinely *new* review issues appeared since the
   485→    last triage.  Triage stage IDs being in the queue alone is not
   486→    sufficient — the new issues that triggered injection may have been
   487→    resolved since then.
   488→
   489→    When issues are merely resolved (current IDs are a subset of
   490→    previously triaged IDs), triage is NOT stale — the user is working
   491→    through the plan.
   492→    """
   493→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   494→    return stale_policy_mod.is_triage_stale(plan, state, triage_ids=TRIAGE_IDS)
   495→

   498→class ImportScoresSyncResult:
   499→    """What changed during an import-scores sync."""
   500→
   501→    injected: bool = False
   502→

   507→
   508→def sync_import_scores_needed(
   509→    plan: PlanModel,
   510→    state: StateModel,
   511→    *,
   512→    assessment_mode: str | None = None,
   513→) -> ImportScoresSyncResult:
   514→    """Inject ``workflow::import-scores`` after issues-only import.
   515→
   516→    Only injects when:
   517→    - Assessment mode was ``issues_only`` (scores were skipped)
   518→    - ``workflow::import-scores`` is not already in the queue
   519→    - There are assessments in the payload that could be imported
   520→
   521→    Positioned after score-checkpoint, before create-plan.
   522→    """
   523→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   524→    result = ImportScoresSyncResult()
   525→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   526→
   527→    if WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID in order:
   528→        return result
   529→
   530→    # Only inject when scores were skipped (issues-only mode)
   531→    if assessment_mode != "issues_only":
   532→        return result
   533→
   534→    # Insert after any subjective/workflow items
   535→    insert_at = 0
   536→    for i, fid in enumerate(order):
   537→        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX) or fid.startswith(WORKFLOW_PREFIX):
   538→            insert_at = i + 1
   539→    order.insert(insert_at, WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID)
   540→    result.injected = True
   541→    return result
   542→
   543→
   544→@dataclass
   545→class CommunicateScoreSyncResult:
   546→    """What changed during a communicate-score sync."""
   547→
   548→    injected: bool = False
   549→
   550→    @property
   551→    def changes(self) -> int:
   552→        return int(self.injected)
   553→
   554→
   555→def sync_communicate_score_needed(
   556→    plan: PlanModel,
   557→    state: StateModel,
   558→    *,
   559→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
   560→    scores_just_imported: bool = False,
   561→) -> CommunicateScoreSyncResult:
   562→    """Inject ``workflow::communicate-score`` when scores should be shown.
   563→
   564→    Injects when either:
   565→    - All initial subjective reviews are complete (no unscored dimensions), OR
   566→    - Scores were just imported (trusted/attested/override)
   567→
   568→    And ``workflow::communicate-score`` is not already in the queue.
   569→    Positioned after subjective items but before triage/create-plan.
   570→    """
   571→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   572→    result = CommunicateScoreSyncResult()
   573→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   574→
   575→    # Also treat legacy score-checkpoint as already-present
   576→    if WORKFLOW_COMMUNICATE_SCORE_ID in order or WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID in order:
   577→        return result
   578→
   579→    # Trigger 1: scores just imported
   580→    should_inject = scores_just_imported
   581→
   582→    # Trigger 2: all initial reviews complete (no unscored dimensions)
   583→    if not should_inject:
   584→        if policy is not None:
   585→            should_inject = not policy.unscored_ids
   586→        else:
   587→            should_inject = not current_unscored_ids(state)
   588→
   589→    if not should_inject:
   590→        return result
   591→
   592→    # Insert after any subjective items, before triage/workflow/issues
   593→    insert_at = 0
   594→    for i, fid in enumerate(order):
   595→        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX):
   596→            insert_at = i + 1
   597→    order.insert(insert_at, WORKFLOW_COMMUNICATE_SCORE_ID)
   598→    result.injected = True
   599→    return result
   600→
   601→

   604→    "TRIAGE_ID",
   605→    "TRIAGE_IDS",

1. Add these helper functions BEFORE `sync_issue_clusters` (before line 95):

```python
def _manual_member_ids(clusters: dict) -> set[str]:
    member_ids: set[str] = set()
    for cluster in clusters.values():
        if not cluster.get("auto"):
            member_ids.update(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
    return member_ids


def _group_clusterable_issues(
    issues: dict,
    *,
    manual_member_ids: set[str],
) -> tuple[dict[str, list[str]], dict[str, dict]]:
    groups: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
    issue_data: dict[str, dict] = {}
    for fid, issue in issues.items():
        if issue.get("status") != "open" or issue.get("suppressed") or fid in manual_member_ids:
            continue
        detector = issue.get("detector", "")
        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
        key = _grouping_key(issue, meta)
        if key is None:
            continue
        groups[key].append(fid)
        issue_data[fid] = issue
    filtered = {key: ids for key, ids in groups.items() if len(ids) >= _MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE}
    return filtered, issue_data


def _sync_user_modified_cluster_members(
    plan: dict,
    *,
    clusters: dict,
    existing_name: str,
    member_ids: list[str],
    now: str,
) -> int:
    cluster = clusters[existing_name]
    existing_ids = set(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
    new_ids = [fid for fid in member_ids if fid not in existing_ids]
    changes = 0
    if new_ids:
        cluster["issue_ids"].extend(new_ids)
        cluster["updated_at"] = now
        changes += 1

    overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
    for fid in member_ids:
        if fid not in overrides:
            overrides[fid] = {"issue_id": fid, "created_at": now}
        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = existing_name
        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
    return changes
```

        detector = rep.get("detector", "")

def _subjective_state_sets(
    state: StateModel,
    *,
    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None,
    target_strict: float,
) -> tuple[set[str], set[str], set[str]]:
    if policy is not None:
        unscored = policy.unscored_ids
        stale = policy.stale_ids
        under_target = policy.under_target_ids
    else:
        unscored = current_unscored_ids(state)
        stale = _current_stale_ids(state)
        under_target = current_under_target_ids(state, target_strict=target_strict)
    return unscored, stale, under_target


def _has_objective_backlog(issues: dict, policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None) -> bool:
    if policy is not None:
        return policy.has_objective_backlog
    return any(
        issue.get("status") == "open"
        and issue.get("detector") not in NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
        and not issue.get("suppressed")
        for issue in issues.values()
    )
```

    10→    generate_action as _generate_action,
    11→    generate_description as _generate_description,
    12→    grouping_key as _grouping_key,
    13→)
    14→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import (
    15→    SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX,
    16→    _current_stale_ids,
    17→    current_under_target_ids,
    18→    current_unscored_ids,
    19→)
    20→from desloppify.engine._plan.subjective_policy import (
    21→    NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS,
    22→    SubjectiveVisibility,
    23→)
    24→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    25→
    26→_MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE = 2
    27→_STALE_KEY = "subjective::stale"
    28→_STALE_NAME = "auto/stale-review"
    29→_UNSCORED_KEY = "subjective::unscored"
    30→_UNSCORED_NAME = "auto/initial-review"
    31→_UNDER_TARGET_KEY = "subjective::under-target"
    32→_UNDER_TARGET_NAME = "auto/under-target-review"
    33→_MIN_UNSCORED_CLUSTER_SIZE = 1
    34→
    35→
    36→def _sync_auto_cluster(
    37→    plan: dict,
    38→    clusters: dict,
    39→    existing_by_key: dict[str, str],
    40→    *,
    41→    cluster_key: str,
    42→    cluster_name: str,
    43→    member_ids: list[str],
    44→    description: str,
    45→    action: str,
    46→    now: str,
    47→    optional: bool = False,
    48→) -> int:
    49→    """Create or update an auto-cluster and sync its override entries."""
    50→    changes = 0
    51→    existing_name = existing_by_key.get(cluster_key)
    52→    if existing_name and existing_name in clusters:
    53→        cluster = clusters[existing_name]
    54→        old_ids = set(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
    55→        new_ids_set = set(member_ids)
    56→        if (
    57→            old_ids != new_ids_set
    58→            or cluster.get("description") != description
    59→            or cluster.get("action") != action
    60→        ):
    61→            cluster["issue_ids"] = list(member_ids)
    62→            cluster["description"] = description
    63→            cluster["action"] = action
    64→            cluster["updated_at"] = now
    65→            changes = 1
    66→    else:
    67→        new_cluster = {
    68→            "name": cluster_name,
    69→            "description": description,
    70→            "issue_ids": list(member_ids),
    71→            "created_at": now,
    72→            "updated_at": now,
    73→            "auto": True,
    74→            "cluster_key": cluster_key,
    75→            "action": action,
    76→            "user_modified": False,
    77→        }
    78→        if optional:
    79→            new_cluster["optional"] = True
    80→        clusters[cluster_name] = new_cluster
    81→        existing_by_key[cluster_key] = cluster_name
    82→        changes = 1
    83→
    84→    overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
    85→    current_name = existing_by_key.get(cluster_key, cluster_name)
    86→    for fid in member_ids:
    87→        if fid not in overrides:
    88→            overrides[fid] = {"issue_id": fid, "created_at": now}
    89→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = current_name
    90→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
    91→
    92→    return changes
    93→
    94→
    95→def sync_issue_clusters(
    96→    plan: dict,
    97→    issues: dict,
    98→    clusters: dict,
    99→    existing_by_key: dict[str, str],
   100→    active_auto_keys: set[str],
   101→    now: str,
   102→) -> int:
   103→    """Group open issues by detector/subtype and sync auto-clusters."""
   104→    changes = 0
   105→
   106→    manual_member_ids: set[str] = set()
   107→    for cluster in clusters.values():
   108→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   109→            manual_member_ids.update(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   110→
   111→    groups: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   112→    issue_data: dict[str, dict] = {}
   113→    for fid, issue in issues.items():
   114→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
   115→            continue
   116→        if issue.get("suppressed"):
   117→            continue
   118→        if fid in manual_member_ids:
   119→            continue
   120→
   121→        detector = issue.get("detector", "")
   122→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   123→        key = _grouping_key(issue, meta)
   124→        if key is None:
   125→            continue
   126→
   127→        groups[key].append(fid)
   128→        issue_data[fid] = issue
   129→
   130→    groups = {k: v for k, v in groups.items() if len(v) >= _MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE}
   131→
   132→    for key, member_ids in groups.items():
   133→        active_auto_keys.add(key)
   134→        cluster_name = _cluster_name_from_key(key)
   135→
   136→        rep = issue_data.get(member_ids[0], {})
   137→        detector = rep.get("detector", "")
   138→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   139→        members = [issue_data[fid] for fid in member_ids if fid in issue_data]
   140→
   141→        key_parts = key.split("::")
   142→        subtype = key_parts[2] if len(key_parts) >= 3 else None
   143→
   144→        description = _generate_description(cluster_name, members, meta, subtype)
   145→        action = _generate_action(meta, subtype)
   146→
   147→        existing_name = existing_by_key.get(key)
   148→        if existing_name and existing_name in clusters:
   149→            cluster = clusters[existing_name]
   150→            if cluster.get("user_modified"):
   151→                existing_ids = set(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   152→                new_ids = [fid for fid in member_ids if fid not in existing_ids]
   153→                if new_ids:
   154→                    cluster["issue_ids"].extend(new_ids)
   155→                    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   156→                    changes += 1
   157→                overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
   158→                for fid in member_ids:
   159→                    if fid not in overrides:
   160→                        overrides[fid] = {"issue_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   161→                    overrides[fid]["cluster"] = existing_name
   162→                    overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   163→                continue
   164→
   165→        if cluster_name in clusters and clusters[cluster_name].get("cluster_key") != key:
   166→            cluster_name = f"{cluster_name}-{len(member_ids)}"
   167→
   168→        changes += _sync_auto_cluster(
   169→            plan,
   170→            clusters,
   171→            existing_by_key,
   172→            cluster_key=key,
   173→            cluster_name=cluster_name,
   174→            member_ids=member_ids,
   175→            description=description,
   176→            action=action,
   177→            now=now,
   178→        )
   179→
   180→    return changes
   181→
   182→
   183→def sync_subjective_clusters(
   184→    plan: dict,
   185→    state: StateModel,
   186→    issues: dict,
   187→    clusters: dict,
   188→    existing_by_key: dict[str, str],
   189→    active_auto_keys: set[str],
   190→    now: str,
   191→    *,
   192→    target_strict: float,
   193→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
   194→    cycle_just_completed: bool = False,
   195→) -> int:
   196→    """Sync unscored, stale, and under-target subjective dimension clusters."""
   197→    changes = 0
   198→
   199→    all_subjective_ids = sorted(
   200→        fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
   201→        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   202→    )
   203→
   204→    if policy is not None:
   205→        unscored_state_ids = policy.unscored_ids
   206→        stale_state_ids = policy.stale_ids
   207→    else:
   208→        unscored_state_ids = current_unscored_ids(state)
   209→        stale_state_ids = _current_stale_ids(state)
   210→
   211→    unscored_queue_ids = sorted(
   212→        fid for fid in all_subjective_ids if fid in unscored_state_ids
   213→    )
   214→    stale_queue_ids = sorted(
   215→        fid for fid in all_subjective_ids
   216→        if fid in stale_state_ids and fid not in unscored_state_ids
   217→    )
   218→
   219→    if len(unscored_queue_ids) >= _MIN_UNSCORED_CLUSTER_SIZE:
   220→        active_auto_keys.add(_UNSCORED_KEY)
   221→        cli_keys = [fid.removeprefix(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX) for fid in unscored_queue_ids]
   222→        description = (
   223→            f"Initial review of {len(unscored_queue_ids)} unscored subjective dimensions"
   224→        )
   225→        action = f"desloppify review --prepare --dimensions {','.join(cli_keys)}"
   226→        changes += _sync_auto_cluster(
   227→            plan,
   228→            clusters,
   229→            existing_by_key,
   230→            cluster_key=_UNSCORED_KEY,
   231→            cluster_name=_UNSCORED_NAME,
   232→            member_ids=unscored_queue_ids,
   233→            description=description,
   234→            action=action,
   235→            now=now,
   236→        )
   237→
   238→    if len(stale_queue_ids) >= _MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE:
   239→        active_auto_keys.add(_STALE_KEY)
   240→        cli_keys = [fid.removeprefix(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX) for fid in stale_queue_ids]
   241→        description = f"Re-review {len(stale_queue_ids)} stale subjective dimensions"
   242→        action = "desloppify review --prepare --dimensions " + ",".join(cli_keys)
   243→        changes += _sync_auto_cluster(
   244→            plan,
   245→            clusters,
   246→            existing_by_key,
   247→            cluster_key=_STALE_KEY,
   248→            cluster_name=_STALE_NAME,
   249→            member_ids=stale_queue_ids,
   250→            description=description,
   251→            action=action,
   252→            now=now,
   253→        )
   254→
   255→    if policy is not None:
   256→        under_target_ids = policy.under_target_ids
   257→    else:
   258→        under_target_ids = current_under_target_ids(state, target_strict=target_strict)
   259→    under_target_queue_ids = sorted(under_target_ids)
   260→
   261→    prev_ut_cluster = clusters.get(_UNDER_TARGET_NAME, {})
   262→    prev_ut_ids = set(prev_ut_cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   263→    order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
   264→    ut_prune = [
   265→        fid for fid in prev_ut_ids
   266→        if fid not in under_target_ids
   267→        and fid not in stale_state_ids
   268→        and fid not in unscored_state_ids
   269→        and fid in order
   270→    ]
   271→    for fid in ut_prune:
   272→        order.remove(fid)
   273→        changes += 1
   274→
   275→    if policy is not None:
   276→        has_objective_items = policy.has_objective_backlog
   277→    else:
   278→        has_objective_items = any(
   279→            f.get("status") == "open"
   280→            and f.get("detector") not in NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
   281→            and not f.get("suppressed")
   282→            for f in issues.values()
   283→        )
   284→
   285→    if not has_objective_items and len(under_target_queue_ids) >= _MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE:
   286→        active_auto_keys.add(_UNDER_TARGET_KEY)
   287→        cli_keys = [fid.removeprefix(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX) for fid in under_target_queue_ids]
   288→        description = (
   289→            f"Consider re-reviewing {len(under_target_queue_ids)} "
   290→            f"dimensions under target score"
   291→        )
   292→        action = "desloppify review --prepare --dimensions " + ",".join(cli_keys)
   293→        changes += _sync_auto_cluster(
   294→            plan,
   295→            clusters,
   296→            existing_by_key,
   297→            [REDACTED],
   298→            cluster_name=_UNDER_TARGET_NAME,
   299→            member_ids=under_target_queue_ids,
   300→            description=description,
   301→            action=action,
   302→            now=now,
   303→            optional=True,
   304→        )
   305→
   306→        existing_order = set(order)
   307→        for fid in under_target_queue_ids:
   308→            if fid not in existing_order:
   309→                order.append(fid)
   310→
   311→    if has_objective_items and not cycle_just_completed:
   312→        objective_evict = [
   313→            fid for fid in order
   314→            if fid in under_target_ids
   315→        ]
   316→        for fid in objective_evict:
   317→            order.remove(fid)
   318→            changes += 1
   319→
   320→    return changes
   321→
   322→
   323→def prune_stale_clusters(
   324→    plan: dict,
   325→    issues: dict,
   326→    clusters: dict,
   327→    active_auto_keys: set[str],
   328→    now: str,
   329→) -> int:
   330→    """Delete auto-clusters that no longer have matching groups."""
   331→    changes = 0
   332→    for name in list(clusters.keys()):
   333→        cluster = clusters[name]
   334→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   335→            continue
   336→        ck = cluster.get("cluster_key", "")
   337→        if ck in active_auto_keys:
   338→            continue
   339→        if cluster.get("user_modified"):
   340→            alive = [
   341→                fid for fid in cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
   342→                if fid in issues and issues[fid].get("status") == "open"
   343→            ]
   344→            if alive:
   345→                if len(alive) != len(cluster.get("issue_ids", [])):
   346→                    cluster["issue_ids"] = alive
   347→                    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   348→                    changes += 1
   349→                continue
   350→        del clusters[name]
   351→        for fid in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
   352→            override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(fid)
   353→            if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
   354→                override["cluster"] = None
   355→                override["updated_at"] = now
   356→        if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
   357→            plan["active_cluster"] = None
   358→        changes += 1
   359→    return changes
   360→
   361→
   362→__all__ = ["prune_stale_clusters", "sync_issue_clusters", "sync_subjective_clusters"]
   363→
```

```python
def _build_prod_module_index(production_files: set[str]) -> dict[str, str]:
    prod_by_module: dict[str, str] = {}
    root_str = str(get_project_root()) + os.sep
    for prod_file in production_files:
        rel_file = prod_file[len(root_str) :] if prod_file.startswith(root_str) else prod_file
        module_name = rel_file.replace("/", ".").replace("\\", ".")
        if "." in module_name:
            module_name = module_name.rsplit(".", 1)[0]
        prod_by_module[module_name] = prod_file
        if module_name.endswith(".__init__"):
            prod_by_module[module_name[: -len(".__init__")]] = prod_file
        parts = module_name.split(".")
        if parts:
            prod_by_module[parts[-1]] = prod_file
    return prod_by_module


def _graph_tested_imports(
    graph: dict,
    test_file: str,
    production_files: set[str],
) -> set[str]:
    entry = graph.get(test_file)
    if entry is None:
        return set()
    return {imp for imp in entry.get("imports", set()) if imp in production_files}


def _expand_barrel_targets(
    *,
    tested: set[str],
    barrel_basenames: set[str],
    production_files: set[str],
    lang_name: str | None,
) -> set[str]:
    if not barrel_basenames:
        return set()
    barrel_files = [f for f in tested if os.path.basename(f) in barrel_basenames]
    expanded: set[str] = set()
    for barrel_file in barrel_files:
        expanded |= _resolve_barrel_reexports(barrel_file, production_files, lang_name)
    return expanded


def _expand_facade_targets(
    *,
    tested: set[str],
    graph: dict,
    production_files: set[str],
    has_logic,
) -> set[str]:
    if not callable(has_logic):
        return set()
    facade_targets: set[str] = set()
    for tested_file in list(tested):
        entry = graph.get(tested_file)
        if entry is None:
            continue
        read_result = read_coverage_file(
            tested_file,
            context="coverage_import_mapping_facade_logic",
        )
        if not read_result.ok:
            continue
        if has_logic(tested_file, read_result.content):
            continue
        for imp in entry.get("imports", set()):
            if imp in production_files:
                facade_targets.add(imp)
    return facade_targets
```

    22→def _load_lang_test_coverage_module(lang_name: str | None):
    23→    """Load language-specific test coverage helpers from ``lang/<name>/test_coverage.py``."""
    24→    return get_lang_hook(lang_name, "test_coverage") or object()
    25→
    26→
    27→def _infer_lang_name(test_files: set[str], production_files: set[str]) -> str | None:
    28→    """Infer language from known file extensions when explicit lang is unavailable."""
    29→    paths = list(test_files) + list(production_files)
    30→    ext_to_lang = {
    31→        ".py": "python",
    32→        ".pyi": "python",
    33→        ".ts": "typescript",
    34→        ".tsx": "typescript",
    35→        ".js": "typescript",
    36→        ".jsx": "typescript",
    37→        ".cs": "csharp",
    38→    }
    39→    counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    40→    for file_path in paths:
    41→        suffix = Path(file_path).suffix.lower()
    42→        lang_name = ext_to_lang.get(suffix)
    43→        if not lang_name:
    44→            continue
    45→        counts[lang_name] = counts.get(lang_name, 0) + 1
    46→    if counts:
    47→        return max(counts.items(), key=lambda item: item[1])[0]
    48→    return None
    49→
    50→
    51→def import_based_mapping(
    52→    graph: dict,
    53→    test_files: set[str],
    54→    production_files: set[str],
    55→    lang_name: str | None = None,
    56→) -> set[str]:
    57→    """Map test files to production files via import edges."""
    58→    lang_name = lang_name or _infer_lang_name(test_files, production_files)
    59→    mod = _load_lang_test_coverage_module(lang_name)
    60→
    61→    tested = set()
    62→
    63→    # Build module-name->path index for resolving test imports.
    64→    prod_by_module: dict[str, str] = {}
    65→    root_str = str(get_project_root()) + os.sep
    66→    for pf in production_files:
    67→        rel_pf = pf[len(root_str) :] if pf.startswith(root_str) else pf
    68→        module_name = rel_pf.replace("/", ".").replace("\\", ".")
    69→        if "." in module_name:
    70→            module_name = module_name.rsplit(".", 1)[0]
    71→        prod_by_module[module_name] = pf
    72→
    73→        # __init__.py: also map package path (e.g. "foo.bar" -> __init__.py).
    74→        if module_name.endswith(".__init__"):
    75→            prod_by_module[module_name[: -len(".__init__")]] = pf
    76→
    77→        parts = module_name.split(".")
    78→        if parts:
    79→            prod_by_module[parts[-1]] = pf
    80→
    81→    for tf in test_files:
    82→        entry = graph.get(tf)
    83→        graph_mapped = set()
    84→        if entry is not None:
    85→            for imp in entry.get("imports", set()):
    86→                if imp in production_files:
    87→                    graph_mapped.add(imp)
    88→            tested |= graph_mapped
    89→
    90→        # Parse source imports as a supplement when graph imports are absent,
    91→        # or always for TypeScript where dynamic import('...') is common in
    92→        # coverage smoke tests and may be missed by static graph building.
    93→        if not graph_mapped or lang_name == "typescript":
    94→            tested |= _parse_test_imports(
    95→                tf, production_files, prod_by_module, lang_name
    96→            )
    97→
    98→    barrel_basenames = getattr(mod, "BARREL_BASENAMES", set())
    99→    if barrel_basenames:
   100→        barrel_files = [f for f in tested if os.path.basename(f) in barrel_basenames]
   101→        for bf in barrel_files:
   102→            tested |= _resolve_barrel_reexports(bf, production_files, lang_name)
   103→
   104→    # Facade expansion: if a directly-tested file has no testable logic (pure
   105→    # re-export facade), promote its imports to directly tested.  This prevents
   106→    # false "transitive_only" issues for internal modules behind facades like
   107→    # scoring.py -> _scoring/policy/core.py.
   108→    has_logic = getattr(mod, "has_testable_logic", None)
   109→    if callable(has_logic):
   110→        facade_targets: set[str] = set()
   111→        for f in list(tested):
   112→            entry = graph.get(f)
   113→            if entry is None:
   114→                continue
   115→            read_result = read_coverage_file(

   117→            )
   118→            if not read_result.ok:
   119→                continue
   120→            content = read_result.content
   121→            if not has_logic(f, content):
   122→                for imp in entry.get("imports", set()):
   123→                    if imp in production_files:
   124→                        facade_targets.add(imp)
   125→        tested |= facade_targets
   126→
   127→    return tested
   128→
   129→
   130→def _resolve_import(
   131→    spec: str,
   132→    test_path: str,
   133→    production_files: set[str],
   134→    lang_name: str | None,
   135→) -> str | None:
   136→    mod = _load_lang_test_coverage_module(lang_name)
   137→    resolver = getattr(mod, "resolve_import_spec", None)
   138→    if callable(resolver):
   139→        return resolver(spec, test_path, production_files)
   140→    return None
   141→
   142→
   143→def _resolve_barrel_reexports(
   144→    filepath: str,
   145→    production_files: set[str],
   146→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   147→) -> set[str]:
   148→    """Resolve one-hop re-exports using language-specific helpers."""
   149→    if lang_name is None:
   150→        lang_name = _infer_lang_name({filepath}, production_files)
   151→    mod = _load_lang_test_coverage_module(lang_name)
   152→    resolver = getattr(mod, "resolve_barrel_reexports", None)
   153→    if callable(resolver):
   154→        return resolver(filepath, production_files)
   155→    return set()
   156→
   157→
   158→def _parse_test_imports(
   159→    test_path: str,
   160→    production_files: set[str],
   161→    prod_by_module: dict[str, str],
   162→    lang_name: str | None = None,
   163→) -> set[str]:
   164→    """Parse import statements from a test file and resolve production files."""
   165→    tested = set()
   166→    read_result = read_coverage_file(test_path, context="coverage_import_mapping_parse")
   167→    if not read_result.ok:
   168→        return tested
   169→    content = read_result.content
   170→
   171→    if lang_name is None:
   172→        lang_name = _infer_lang_name({test_path}, production_files)
   173→
   174→    mod = _load_lang_test_coverage_module(lang_name)
   175→    parse_specs = getattr(mod, "parse_test_import_specs", None)
   176→    if not callable(parse_specs):
   177→        return tested
   178→
   179→    for spec in parse_specs(content):
   180→        if not spec:
   181→            continue
   182→
   183→        resolved = _resolve_import(spec, test_path, production_files, lang_name)
   184→        if resolved:
   185→            tested.add(resolved)
   186→            continue
   187→
   188→        # Fallback: module-name lookup with progressively shorter prefixes.
   189→        cleaned = spec.lstrip("./").replace("/", ".")
   190→        parts = cleaned.split(".")
   191→        for i in range(len(parts), 0, -1):
   192→            candidate = ".".join(parts[:i])
   193→            if candidate in prod_by_module:
   194→                tested.add(prod_by_module[candidate])
   195→                break
   196→
   197→    return tested
   198→
   199→
   200→def _map_test_to_source(
   201→    test_path: str,
   202→    production_set: set[str],
   203→    lang_name: str,
   204→) -> str | None:
   205→    """Match a test file to a production file using language conventions."""
   206→    mod = _load_lang_test_coverage_module(lang_name)
   207→    mapper = getattr(mod, "map_test_to_source", None)
   208→    if callable(mapper):
   209→        return mapper(test_path, production_set)
   210→    return None
   211→
   212→
   213→def naming_based_mapping(
   214→    test_files: set[str],
   215→    production_files: set[str],
   216→    lang_name: str,
   217→) -> set[str]:
   218→    """Map test files to production files by naming conventions."""
   219→    tested = set()
   220→
   221→    prod_by_basename: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   222→    for p in production_files:
   223→        bn = os.path.basename(p)
   224→        prod_by_basename.setdefault(bn, []).append(p)
   225→
   226→    for tf in test_files:
   227→        matched = _map_test_to_source(tf, production_files, lang_name)
   228→        if matched:
   229→            tested.add(matched)
   230→            continue
   231→
   232→        basename = os.path.basename(tf)
   233→        src_name = _strip_test_markers(basename, lang_name)
   234→        if src_name and src_name in prod_by_basename:
   235→            for p in prod_by_basename[src_name]:
   236→                tested.add(p)
   237→
   238→    return tested
   239→
   240→
   241→def _strip_test_markers(basename: str, lang_name: str) -> str | None:
   242→    """Strip test naming markers from a basename to derive source basename."""
   243→    mod = _load_lang_test_coverage_module(lang_name)
   244→    strip_markers = getattr(mod, "strip_test_markers", None)
   245→    if callable(strip_markers):
   246→        return strip_markers(basename)
   247→    return None

   250→def transitive_coverage(

   256→    return transitive_coverage_core(directly_tested, graph, production_files)

   259→def analyze_test_quality(
   260→    test_files: set[str],

   268→        read_coverage_file_fn=read_coverage_file,
